a_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}a_{1}
, note that
a_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
and
a_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}a_{2}
, by Lemma
5.12
(ii), imply
a_{1}\equiv a_{2}\,(Ab)
.

(2)
\Rightarrow
(3) Assume (2). Toward a contradiction, suppose
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
; in particular,
b\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
. Choose
a^{\prime}\models p
such that
b\notin\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
; this is possible as
p\nwor q
is valid. Then
b\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)\smallsetminus\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
are
\subseteq
-comparable (as the final parts of
(p(\Mon),<_{p})
), we conclude
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)\supsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
, so
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}
follows by (2); in particular,
a<_{p}a^{\prime}
. As
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}}(b)
is a final part of
(\Mon)
,
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a<_{p}a^{\prime}
implies
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}b
and thus
a\equiv a^{\prime}\ (Ab)
; this is in contradiction with
b\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)\smallsetminus\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
.

(3)
\Leftrightarrow\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
follows from Lemma
6.1
.
∎

In part (i) of the lemma below, we prove a general form of transitivity of
\triangleleft
; further in the text, we will refer to it as the transitivity property.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})
and
\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})
be so-pairs over
A
with
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})

(i)

For all
a,b
and
B
:
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\land a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
.

(ii)

For all
a\models p,b\models q
and all
B
:
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
.

Proof.

(i) Suppose not. Then
b\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)\smallsetminus\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
are the final parts of
(q(\Mon),<_{q})
we get
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)\subsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
. Let
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
. Choose
a^{\prime}\models p
such that
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
. Then
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})<_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
and

b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
together imply

b^{\prime}<_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
, which combined with
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
gives

\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})\subsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
. Therefore,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})\subsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)\subsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
holds and, in particular,
a\nequiv a^{\prime}\ (AB)
. By Lemma
6.5
(2),
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)\supsetneq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(a^{\prime})
implies
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}
, which together with

B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}
. Consequently,
a,a^{\prime}\models(\mathbf{p}_{r})_{\restriction AB}
, which is in contradiction with
a\nequiv a^{\prime}\,(AB)
.

(ii) Assume that
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
,
b\models q
, and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
. Then
b\in\mathcal{D}_{q}(a)
. By the density property of
\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}
, there exists
b^{\prime}
such that
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
. Now,
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
implies that option (I) of Lemma
6.1
(i) holds, so
b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
implies
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
which combined with
b\in\mathcal{D}_{q}(a)
yields
b^{\prime}<_{q}b
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{q}}(B)
is a final part of
q(\Mon)
,
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime}<_{q}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
, completing the proof of (ii).
∎

As we have already remarked, a so-type need not be NIP. However, so-types share the following property of stationary, weight-one types in stable theories:

Proposition 6.7
.

Assume that
p,q\in S(A)
are so-types.

(i)

If
p\nwor q
, then
p\wor r\Leftrightarrow q\wor r
for all
r\in S(A)
.

(ii)

If
p\nfor q
, then
p\fwor r\Leftrightarrow q\fwor r
for all
r\in S(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})
and
\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})
be so-pairs over
A
such that
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
.

(i) Assuming
p\nwor q
and
p\nwor r
we will prove
q\nwor r
. Let
C\models r
. As
p\nwor r
implies that
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{p}}(C)
is a proper final part of
p(\Mon)
, there is an
a\models p
with
a<_{p}\mathcal{R}_{p}(C)
.
Choose
a^{\prime}\models p
and
b,b^{\prime}\models q
such that:

b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a<_{p}\mathcal{R}_{p}(C)
and
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime}
;

This is possible because of the existence of generic elements.
We will prove
b\nequiv b^{\prime}\,(AC)
; clearly, this implies
q\nwor r
.
Note that
b
satisfies the following (infinitary) formula:
(\exists x\models p)(x<\mathcal{R}_{p}(C)\land b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}x)
. Hence, if
b\equiv b^{\prime}\,(C)
were valid, there would be a
AC
-automorphism moving
b
to
b^{\prime}
and there would be an
a^{\prime\prime}\models p
with
a^{\prime\prime}<_{p}\mathcal{R}_{p}(C)
and
b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime\prime}
. Then
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime\prime}
would imply
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime\prime}
and hence
a^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{p}(C)
; contradiction. Therefore,
b\nequiv b^{\prime}\,(AC)
, completing the proof.

(ii) Assuming
p\nfor q
and
p\nfor r
we will prove
q\nfor r
. Let
C\models r
. Choose
a\models p
and
b\models q
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}C
. We will prove
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}C
, which implies the desired conclusion. If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}C
, then after possibly reversing the orders
<_{p}
and
<_{q}
, we may assume
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
. Then
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
holds, so Lemma
6.6
(ii) implies
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
, which contradicts the initial assumption
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}C
. Therefore,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}C
.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})
and
\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})
be so-pairs over
A
with
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
. Then for all
B
,
a\models p
and
b\models q
:

(i)

(Existence) There exists a
a^{\prime}\models p
such that
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}
;

(ii)

(Density) If
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
, then there is a
a^{\prime}\models p
such that
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
;

(iii)

(Symmetry)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
if and only if
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}a
;

(iv)

a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b\Leftrightarrow b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}^{*}}a
(
b
is right
\mathbf{q}
-generic over
a
if and only if
a
is left
\mathbf{p}
-generic over
b
.)

(v)

(
\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}
-comparability)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}B\Leftrightarrow(B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\vee B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}^{*}}a)

and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b\Leftrightarrow(a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b\vee b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a)
;

(vi)

(Transitivity)
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
.

(vii)

B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
; that is,
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}x
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{q}}(a)
.

(viii)

a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
if and only if
x\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}y
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{p}}(a)
and
y\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{q}}(b)
.

Proof.

(i)-(vii) follow easily from previous results; for example, (vi)-(vii) follow from Lemma
6.6
. To prove (viii), assume
a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{p}(a)
, and
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{q}(b)
. Then:

(a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b)\Leftrightarrow(a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime})\Leftrightarrow(b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}^{*}}a)\Leftrightarrow(b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}^{*}}a^{\prime})\Leftrightarrow(a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime})\ ;

where the first and third equivalences are consequences of (vii), and the second and fourth follow by (iv). This proves (viii).
∎

Recall that the pairs
(p,<_{p})
and
(q,<_{q})
are weakly nonorthogonal if
p\nwor q
.

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{A}
denote the set of all so-types over
A
and
\mathcal{P}_{A}
the set of all so-pairs over
A
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{A}(\Mon)
be the set of all realizations of types from
\mathcal{S}_{A}
.

(i)

\nwor
is an equivalence relation on both
\mathcal{S}_{A}
and
\mathcal{P}_{A}
.

(ii)

\delta_{A}
is an equivalence relation on
\mathcal{P}_{A}
;
\delta_{A}
refines
\nwor
by splitting each class into two classes, each of them consisting of the reverses of pairs from the other class.

(iii)

x\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}y
is an equivalence relation on
\mathcal{S}_{A}(\Mon)
.

(iv)

\nfor
is an equivalence relation on
\mathcal{S}_{A}
.

Proof.

Clearly, both
\delta_{A}
and
\nwor
are reflexive and symmetric.

(i) The transitivity of
\nwor
is a consequence of Proposition
6.7
.

(ii) To prove the transitivity of
\delta_{A}
, assume that
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})
,
\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})
and
\mathbf{r}=(r,<_{r})
are so-pairs such that
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
and
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{r})
. By part (i),
p\nwor q
and
q\nwor r

imply
p\nwor r
. To prove
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r})
, we will verify the equivalent condition (1) of Lemma
6.5
: Assuming
a_{1}<_{p}a_{2}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{r}}c
, we need to prove
a_{1}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{r}}c
. By the density property, there exists a
b\models q
that satisfies
a_{2}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{r}}c
. Then
a_{1}<_{p}a_{2}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
, by condition (1) from Lemma
6.5
, implies
a_{1}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
. By Lemma
6.6
(i),
a_{1}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{r}}c
implies
a_{1}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{r}}c
, as desired. Therefore,
\delta_{A}
is an equivalence relation. To show that there are exactly two
\delta_{A}
classes, recall Remark
6.3
(b): for all
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}
over
A
,
p\nwor q
implies
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\vee\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}^{*})
. Hence, if
p
is non-algebraic, then the
\nwor
-class of
\mathbf{p}
is split into two
\delta_{A}
-classes.

(iii) By Lemma
5.11
,
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}
is symmetric and the reflexivity is clear.
To prove transitivity, assume
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}c
, and we prove
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}c
. Let
p=\tp(a/A),q=\tp(b/A)
and
r=\tp(c/A)
; these are so-types, and note that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
p\nwor q
. Thus, if
p\wor r
, then
q\wor r
holds by (i);
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}c
follows, and we are done.
So, assume
p\nwor r
. By (i),
p,q
, and
r
are in the same
\nwor
-class, so we can choose
orders
<_{p},<_{q}
and
<_{r}
such that the corresponding so-pairs
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}
and
\mathbf{r}
are in the same
\delta_{A}
-class.
Then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}c
implies that
a
is left- or right
\mathbf{p}
-generic over
c
; without loss (by reversing all three orders if necessary) assume
c\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
.
Thus, we have:
c\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
which, by Lemma
6.6
(ii) implies
c\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
and therefore
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}c
, as needed.

(iv) Follows from (iii).
∎

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a set of pairwise directly non-orthogonal so-pairs over
A
. By Theorem
6.9
,
\mathcal{F}
is part of a
\delta_{A}
-class of so-pairs over
A
. We will use the following notation:

\mathcal{F}(\Mon)=\bigcup\{p(\Mon)\mid(p,<_{p})\in\mathcal{F}\mbox{ for some $<_{p}$}\}\ \ \ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \ \ \mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}=\{(a,b)\in\mathcal{F}(\Mon)\times\mathcal{F}(\Mon)\mid a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b\};

Here,
\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
is the set of realizations of all types (appearing in some pair) from
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}
is the equivalence relation of forking on
\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
. Also, define
\mathcal{F}^{*}
, called the reverse of
\mathcal{F}
, by
\mathcal{F}^{*}=\{\mathbf{p}^{*}\mid\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{F}\}
. Note that, by Theorem
6.9
(ii),
\mathcal{F}^{*}
is also a part of a
\delta_{A}
-class.
In particular, if
\mathcal{F}
is the whole
\delta_{A}
-class, then the union
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{F}^{*}
is a
\nwor
-class of so-pairs from
\mathcal{P}_{A}
.

Definition 6.10
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a part of a
\delta_{A}
-class of so-pairs over
A
and let
a\in\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
. We say that
a
is right
\mathcal{F}
-generic over
B
, denoted by
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a
, if
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
holds for some (equivalently, any
2
2

2

By Lemma
6.4
) pair
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})\in\mathcal{F}
such that
a\models p
.

In the next theorem, we collect the basic properties of the relation
\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}
for so-types.

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be part of a
\delta_{A}
-class of so-pairs over
A
. Then for all small
B
, all
\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{F}
, and all
a,b\in\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
the following hold true:

(i)

(Existence) There exists a
c\models p
such that
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}c
;

(ii)

(Density) If
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
, then there is a
c
such that
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}c\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
;

(iii)

(Symmetry)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
if and only if
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}a
;

(iv)

a\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b\Leftrightarrow b\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}^{*}}a
(
b
is right
\mathcal{F}
-generic over
a
if and only if
a
is left
\mathcal{F}
-generic over
b
.)

(v)

(
\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}
-comparability)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}B\Leftrightarrow(B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a\vee B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}^{*}}a)

and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b\Leftrightarrow(a\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b\vee b\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a)
;

(vi)

(Transitivity)
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
.

(vii)

B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
; that is,
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}x
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}(a)
.

(viii)

a\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
if and only if
x\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}y
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}(a)
and
y\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}(b)
.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
6.8
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
\delta_{A}
-class and let
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})\in\mathcal{F}
. By part Theorem
6.11
(v), it follows that
(\mathcal{F}(\Mon),\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}})
is a strict partial order. By the definition of
\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}
, the restriction of this order to
p(\Mon)
is the order
(p(\Mon),\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}})
, which has a linear extension
(p(\Mon),<_{p})
.

•

Let
(p_{i}\mid i<\alpha)
be an enumeration of all (pairwise distinct) types
p\in S(A)
appearing in
\mathcal{F}
;

•

For each
i<\alpha
choose
<_{i}
such that
(p_{i},<_{i})\in\mathcal{F}
.

In order to linearize
\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}
, by Theorem
6.11
(viii), it suffices to linearize each class
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}(a)
(
a\in\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
). This is easy to do in an
A
-invariant way. For example:

x<^{\mathcal{F}}y
if and only if
x\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}y
or (
x\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}y
,
x\models p_{i},y\models p_{j}
and
i<j
).

It is straightforward to verify that the order
<^{\mathcal{F}}
satisfies the conclusion of Theorem
5
.
∎

6.1.
Orientation of extensions of weakly o-minimal pairs

Throughout this subsection, let
\mathcal{F}
be a part of a
\delta_{A}
-class of weakly o-minimal pairs over
A
; as weakly o-minimal types are so-types, the conclusion of Theorem
5
still holds, so let
<^{\mathcal{F}}
be an
A
-invariant order on
\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
such that (i)-(iii) of Theorem
5
are satisfied.
Since complete extensions of weakly o-minimal types are also weakly o-minimal, it makes sense to consider nonforking extensions of types (pairs) from
\mathcal{F}
over a larger domain
B\supseteq A
. For each
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})\in\mathcal{F}
define:

p_{B}=(\mathbf{p}_{r})_{\restriction B}
,
\mathbf{p}_{B}=(p_{B},<_{p})
,
\mathcal{F}_{B}=\{\mathbf{p}_{B}\mid\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{F}\}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)=\bigcup_{\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{F}}p_{B}(\Mon)
.

Proposition 6.12
.

For all
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in\mathcal{F}
and all
a,b,c\in\mathcal{F}(\Mon)
:

(i)

For all
C
:
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a\Leftrightarrow\ BC\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
. In particular,
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a
implies
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
for all
b\models q_{B}
;

(ii)

\delta_{B}(\mathbf{p}_{B},\mathbf{q}_{B})
holds;

(iii)

B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
(
a\in\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
implies
b\in\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
);

(iv)

B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
implies
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
(
a\in\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)\land a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
implies
b\in\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)
);

(v)

B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b\land\,a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
implies
Ba\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
;

(vi)

B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b<^{\mathcal{F}}c\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}c
implies
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}a\land b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}c
;

Proof.

(i) Clearly:
C\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a\ \Leftrightarrow\ a\models(\mathbf{p}_{r})_{\restriction BC}\ \Leftrightarrow\ BC\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
. In particular, if
b\models q_{B}
and
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a
, then
Bb\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
and hence
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
.

(ii) By Proposition
6.7
(i) we have
p_{B}\nwor q_{B}
.
To prove
\delta_{B}(\mathbf{p}_{B},\mathbf{q}_{B})
, by Lemma
6.5
it suffices to show that
a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}_{B}}b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a^{\prime}
cannot hold for some
a^{\prime}\models p_{B}
and
b^{\prime}\models q_{B}
. If it did, then
a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}_{B}}b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a^{\prime}
, by part (i), would lead to
a^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b^{\prime}\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a^{\prime}
, which would contradict
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
.

(iii) Suppose that
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a\land a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
. Choose
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p}),\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})\in\mathcal{F}
such that
p=\tp(a/A)
,
q=\tp(b/A)
. By Theorem
6.11
(vii),
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
imply
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
(
b\models q_{B}
), proving the first part of the claim. To prove the second,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
, first note that
a\models p_{B}
and
b\models q_{B}
, so if
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
were true, then we would have
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}_{B}}b
or
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a
. By (i), this would imply
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
or
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
, which would contradict
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
. Therefore,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
.

(iv) Suppose that
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
. By Theorem
5
(i)-(ii)
a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
implies
(a,b)\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}
or
a\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
. In the first case,
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
follows by Theorem
6.11
(vii), and in the second by the transitivity property of
\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}
.

(v) Suppose that
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
. Denote
p=\tp(a/A)
,
q=\tp(b/A)
and choose
\mathbf{p}=(p,<_{p})
,
\mathbf{q}=(q,<_{q})\in\mathcal{F}
. We have:
a\models p_{B}
,
b\models q_{B}
(by (iv)), and
\delta_{B}(\mathbf{p}_{B},\mathbf{q}_{B})
(by (ii)). Now,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
implies
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}_{B}}b
or
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a
. By (i),
b\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}_{B}}a
implies
b\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a
, which contradicts
a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
. Therefore,
a\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}_{B}}b
holds, so
b\models\mathbf{q}_{Ba}
, that is,
Ba\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
.

(vi) Assume that
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b<^{\mathcal{F}}c
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}c
. We will prove
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
; then
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}c
follows by the transitivity of
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}
.
If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
does not hold, then according to (v),
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
and
B\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}a<^{\mathcal{F}}b
would imply
Ba\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}b
. This together with
b\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}c
would lead to
Ba\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}}c
, contradicting
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}c
. Thus, we must have
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
.
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

Let
<^{\mathcal{F}_{B}}:=<^{\mathcal{F}}{\restriction\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}}:=\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)^{2}\mid x\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}y\}
.

(i)

\mathcal{F}_{B}
is a part of some
\delta_{B}
-class and
\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)
is a final part of
(\mathcal{F}(\Mon),<^{\mathcal{F}})
;

(ii)

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}}
is a convex equivalence relation on
(\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon),<^{\mathcal{F}_{B}})
that is coarser than
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}\restriction\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)
;

(iii)

The structure
(\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon),\triangleleft^{\mathcal{F}_{B}},<^{\mathcal{F}_{B}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}})
satisfies the conclusion of Theorem
5
.

Proof.

(i)
\mathcal{F}_{B}
is a part of some
\delta_{B}
-class by Proposition
6.12
(ii);
\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)
is a final part of
(\mathcal{F}(\Mon),<^{\mathcal{F}_{B}})
by Proposition
6.12
(iii).

(ii)
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}_{B}}
is convex by Proposition
6.12
(vii); it is coarser than
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{F}}\restriction\mathcal{F}_{B}(\Mon)
by Proposition
6.12
(iii).

(iii) Straightforward.
∎

Now we drop the initial assumptions and formulate the next corollary in a general context.

Corollary 6.14
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}
are weakly o-minimal pairs over
A
with
\delta_{A}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})
. Let
B\supseteq A
. Define
p_{B}=(\mathbf{p}_{r})_{\restriction B}
,
\mathbf{p}_{B}=(p_{B},<_{p})
and analogously define
q_{B}
and
\mathbf{q}_{B}
.

(i)

\delta_{B}(\mathbf{p}_{B},\mathbf{q}_{B})
holds; in particular,
p_{B}\nwor q_{B}
and
\mathbf{p}_{r}\nwor\mathbf{q}_{r}
;

(ii)

If
p\nfor q
, then
p_{B}\nfor q_{B}
and, in particular,
\mathbf{p}_{r}\nfor\mathbf{q}_{r}
.

Proof.

(i) follows from Proposition
6.12
(ii). To prove (ii), assume that
p\nfor q
and let
a\models p_{B}
. Choose
b\models q
with
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
. Then
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{p}}a
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{A}b
, by Proposition
6.12
(ii), imply
B\triangleleft^{\mathbf{q}}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
. Hence
a\models p_{B},b\models q_{B}
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{B}b
;
p_{B}\nfor q_{B}
follows.
∎

7.
dp-minimality

In this section, we prove that weakly o-minimal types are dp-minimal and that their indiscernible sequences enjoy some nice properties. For example, in Proposition
7.8
below, we prove that every Morley sequence
I
of realizations of a weakly o-minimal type
p
remains Morley after replacing every element
a\in I
with an element
a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{p}(a)
.

We will very briefly recall the notions of NIP and dp-minimality for (partial) types, without recalling the original definition of dp-minimal types, as we will not use it in this paper; a detailed exposition of NIP can be found in Simon’s book
[
18
]
. Rather, we recall two characterizations of NIP types (see
[
9
, Claim 2.1]
), since we will use them later. We also recall an equivalent characterization of dp-minimality due to Kaplan, Onshuus, and Usvyatsov (see
[
8
, Proposition 2.8]
).

Definition 7.1
.

Let
p(x)
be a (partial) type over
A
. The type
p(x)
is:

(a)

NIP
if there does
not
exist a formula
\varphi(x,y)
, an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{n}\mid n<\omega)
of tuples of length
|y|
and
a\models p
such that
\models\varphi(a,b_{n})
if and only if
n
is even.
Equivalently, there is no formula
\varphi(x,y)
, an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(a_{n}\mid n<\omega)
in
p
and a tuple
b
, such that
\models\varphi(a_{n},b)
iff
n
is even;

(b)

dp-minimal
if there does
not
exist a formula
\varphi(x,y)
, an infinite
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i}\mid i\in I)
of tuples of length
|y|
, and
a\models p
such that the truth value
\varphi(a,y)
has at least four alternations on
(b_{i}\mid i\in I)
, that is, there are
i_{0}<i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}
such that

\models\varphi(a,b_{i_{j}})\not\leftrightarrow\varphi(c,b_{i_{j+1}})
for all
j<4
.

Fact 7.2
.

(i)

Every dp-minimal type is NIP.

(ii)

Every weakly o-minimal type is NIP.

(iii)

If
p(x)
over
A
is NIP and
a_{i}\models p
for
i<n
, then
\tp(a_{0},\dots,a_{n-1}/A)
is NIP.

Proof.

(i) is obvious by the first characterization from the definition of NIP types. (ii) If
(p,<)
is a weakly o-minimal pair over
A
, then every
A
-indiscernible sequence of realizations of
p
is monotone with respect to
<
, so weak o-minimality implies that the second characterization of NIP types above is satisfied. (iii) follows from
[
8
, Theorem 4.11]
.
∎

Goodrick in
[
6
, Proposition 4.9]
proved that every weakly quasi-o-minimal theory is dp-minimal. We will prove that every partial type whose all completions are weakly o-minimal is dp-minimal and, as a corollary, derive Goodrick’s result. In the proof, we will use the following simple, Helly-style fact.

Fact 7.3
.

Let
(X,<)
be a linear order and
\{S_{n}\mid n<\omega\}
a family of non-empty subsets of
X
such that there is
N<\omega
such that each
S_{n}
has at most
N
convex components. Suppose that
\{S_{n}\mid n<\omega\}
has the
2
-intersection property. Then there is an infinite
I\subseteq\omega
such that
\{S_{n}\mid n\in I\}
has the finite intersection property.

Proof.

Without loss we may assume that each
S_{n}
has
N
convex components, and write
S_{n}=C_{n}^{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\dots\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}C_{n}^{N}
with
C_{n}^{1}<\dots<C_{n}^{N}
being convex. For
n<m
choose minimal
i
and minimal
j
such that
C_{n}^{i}\cap C_{m}^{j}\neq\emptyset
. By Ramsey’s theorem, there is an infinite
I\subseteq\omega
such that the chosen pairs
(i,j)
are the same for all
n<m
in
I
. If
i=j
then
\{C_{n}^{i}\mid n\in I\}
is a
2
-consistent family of convex sets, so it is
k
-consistent for all
k\geqslant 2
by Helly’s theorem, and hence
\{S_{n}\mid n\in I\}
is
k
-consistent for all
k\geqslant 2
, too.

Consider the case
i<j
. If
n<m
in
I
then
C_{m}^{i}<C_{n}^{i}
as
C_{m}^{i}<C_{m}^{j}
and
j
is minimal such that
C_{n}^{i}\cap C_{m}^{j}\neq\emptyset
. Write
I=\{n_{0},n_{1},n_{2},\dots\}
in increasing order. Then
C_{n_{2}}^{i}<C_{n_{1}}^{i}<C_{n_{0}}^{i}
. For each
l\geqslant 3
, the set
C_{n_{l}}^{j}
meets both
C_{n_{2}}^{i}
and
C_{n_{0}}^{i}
, so by convexity it completely contains
C_{n_{1}}^{i}
. Therefore,
\{S_{n_{l}}\mid l\geqslant 3\}
contains
C_{n_{1}}^{i}
at its intersection, so it is
k
-consistent for all
k\geqslant 2
. The proof in the case
i>j
is similar.
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

Suppose that
p(x)
is a partial type over
A
whose completions over
A
are all weakly o-minimal. Then
p(x)
is dp-minimal.

Proof.

Suppose not. Let
\varphi(x,y)
be a formula,
I=(b_{n}\mid n<\omega)
a
A
-indiscernible sequence, and
c
realizing
p
such that
\varphi(c,y)
has at least three alternations on
I
. Let
i_{0}<i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}
be such that:

(
*
)

\models\varphi(c,b_{i_{0}})\land\lnot\varphi(c,b_{i_{1}})\land\varphi(c,b_{i_{2}})\land\lnot\varphi(c,b_{i_{3}}).

Put
q=\tp(c/A)
. By assumption,
q
is a weakly o-minimal type, so
(q,<_{q})
is a weakly o-minimal pair over
A
for some (any) relatively
A
-definable order
<_{q}
on
q(\Mon)
.
Consider the relatively definable subsets
S_{n}
of
q(\Mon)
relatively defined by
\varphi(x,b_{2n})\land\lnot\varphi(x,b_{2n+1})
. By weak o-minimality of
q
and indiscernibility of
I
, for some
N<\omega
each
S_{n}
has
N

<_{q}
-convex components. Furthermore, by the indiscernibility of
I
and (
*
) we see that
\{S_{n}\mid n<\omega\}
is 2-consistent. Then by Fact
7.3
there is an infinite
J\subseteq\omega
such that
\{S_{n}\mid n\in J\}
has the finite intersection property; in fact, by the indiscernibility of
I
, we may conclude that
\{S_{n}\mid n<\omega\}
has the finite intersection property. By saturation we find
a\in\bigcap_{n<\omega}S_{n}
, but this contradicts the fact that
p
is NIP (Fact
7.2
(ii)).
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

Every weakly o-minimal type is dp-minimal, and every weakly quasi-o-minimal theory is dp-minimal.

In the rest of this section, we focus on indiscernible sequences of realizations of a weakly o-minimal type. In part (ii) of the following lemma, we show that they have a bit stronger property than the distality as defined by Simon in
[
19
]
(see
[
19
, Lemma 2.7, Corollary 2.9]
and
[
4
, Definition 4.21]
).

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
(p,<)
be a weakly o-minimal pair over
A
, let
a_{0},a_{1}\models p
and
B\supseteq A
. Suppose that
I
and
J
are sequences of realizations of
p
such that
I+J
is infinite and
<
-increasing. Then:

(i)

If
I
has no maximum,
I<a_{0}<a_{1}
and
I+a_{1}
is
B
-indiscernible, then
I+a_{0}
is
B
-indiscernible.

(ii)

If
I+J
is
B
-indiscernible,
I
has no maximum,
J
has no minimum, and
I<a_{0}<J
, then
I+a_{0}+J
is
B
-indiscernible.

(iii)

If
I+a_{0}+a_{1}+J
is
B
-indiscernible,
a\models p
and
a_{0}<a<a_{1}
, then at least one of the sequences
I+a_{0}+a+J
and
I+a+a_{1}+J
is
B
-indiscernible.

Proof.

(i) Let
I_{0}
be a finite subset of
I
. Since
I
has no maximum, there exists
a\in I
such that
I_{0}<a
. Then
a<a_{0}<a_{1}
and the sequence
I_{0}+a+a_{1}
is
B
-indiscernible, so
a\equiv a_{1}\ (BI_{0})
holds. Since
p
is weakly o-minimal, the locus of type
\tp(a_{1}/BI_{0})
is a convex subset of
(p(\Mon),<)
by Lemma
3.6
(ii), so
a<a_{0}<a_{1}
implies
a_{0}\equiv a_{1}\ (BI_{0})
